# Review log: Runtime SDKs assume one session per process

## Standing context

### Settled

- Owner decisions (gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md section 10.3): one runtime lifetime contract; `session_start` and `session_end` ride CH-MSGSOCK rather than CH-RUNTIMEOPS; no runtime capability declaration for sequential sessions; no backward-compatibility shims. Do not reopen.
- Owner answers of 2026-10-03: Part B (SPEC-8, CODE-7, the nonce handshake) stays here; `session_start` omits `taskId` and the workspace path because both derive from `sessionId`; per-session platform and connector MCP connections, and the "MCP tools stop working for a later session" symptom, belong to proposal 0084; this proposal's spec steps land before 0087's and 0084's. Do not reopen.
- The first-session manifest-ordering defect (BUILD-GAPS F-4.7.26) belongs to draft proposal 0087's supervisor. Stage no fix here; state the dependency only.
- "Stream" in the §28.5.3 card means one session's logical stream multiplexed over the pod's one connection. The carrier is "the connection or loop" (sidecar connection, embedded runtime loop). Never write "stream" for the carrier (spec.1.fix-G1).
- Owner decision of 2026-10-04, cross-channel session ordering: `session_start` is acknowledged. A runtime that keeps per-session context, or that has opened `CH-RUNTIMEOPS`, writes `session_started` on CH-MSGSOCK once the session's context exists, with `error` when creation failed (SPEC-3 Edit 16 **Outbound: `session_started`**, **Session errors.**). The adapter writes no session-scoped CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame for a session before it reads that frame (SPEC-4 Edit 2, the rule's one home). When the runtime's CH-RUNTIMEOPS handshake has completed, the adapter waits for the frame inside the open sequence; a missing or errored acknowledgement writes `session_end`, takes no record, and fails the start on the existing error arm (SPEC-3 **Session frame writes.** acknowledgement-failure row). The adapter does not wait before the first `message`, because CH-MSGSOCK write order already orders it. `session_end` is not acknowledged (summary decision 9). The runtime-side hold-and-drop rule and the SDK wait options are removed. The conformance precondition is "read `session_started` before a session-scoped CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame" (SPEC-6 Edit 4), and the Full **CH-RUNTIMEOPS opening** row checks the acknowledgement (SPEC-6 Edit 5). The adapter-side wait and gate are CODE-9, which lands after CODE-4, CODE-5, and CODE-6. Do not reopen.
- Owner decision of 2026-10-04, `DemoteSDK` fails closed: when the `DemoteSDK` handler's wait for the slot serialization outlasts its request's deadline, it fails without writing `session_end` and tears nothing down; under the existing spec the pod then fails and a replacement is claimed (SPEC-3 Edit 12 exception, **Session frame writes.** `DemoteSDK` deadline row, CODE-1 item 3). Do not reopen.
- The CODE-7 **Client inventory.** table is the one list of in-tree dialers and acceptors of `CH-MSGSOCK` and `CH-RUNTIMEOPS`. A change to a caller edits its row; do not reintroduce a prose caller list in the Targets, item 5, item 7, or the Tests paragraph.

### Traps

- A `*spec-changes.md` glob also matches `*non-spec-changes.md`. Name the file in full when reading, grepping, or editing either one.
- Heartbeat escalation ends only that session's stream (pkg/adapter/heartbeat.go, attach.go hbHung case) and never closes the pod connection (§4.7.10 "No session's teardown, interrupt, or heartbeat escalation ... closes that connection"). §15.4.3 says a missed ack "ends the session"; keep the frame-writes table consistent with both.

### Open

### Deferred

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: MCP dial ordering is per session, after `session_start` (Option B): §4.7.9 step 8 loses "connects to MCP servers" (SPEC-1 Edit 3a), new step 10 gains it after the `session_start` write, step 7 names `CH-MSGSOCK` and `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` as the reused connections, and §29.2 26a/26b move after new step 30 as 30a/30b ("On each session") — BECAUSE the adapter re-arms the pod MCP surface with a fresh manifest nonce on every solo claim and cancels it at runtime idle (pkg/adapter/slotsession.go claimPodMCPStartLocked, cancelPodMCPIfRuntimeIdle; pkg/adapter/manifest.go writeSessionManifest), so a process-held MCP connection points at a retired surface — ALTERNATIVES: once-per-process MCP dial (re-creates the defect the problem statement names); keeping 26a/26b in place with a forward reference (numbered order disagrees with time order).
WATCHOUT: §29.2 26a carries the tier-11 nonce gate anchor "The runtime reads the manifest and connects to the platform MCP" — move the step, never reword that sentence. DECISION: `session_start` rule 1 is the single home of session_start write ordering, now including "before the §4.7.1 rule-8 confirmation" — BECAUSE only then does the rule-8 rollback's `session_end` land after the `session_start` it releases; a racing teardown's `session_end` before it is ignored under session_end rule 3. DECISION: SDK MCP dial is per session after session_start (summary decision 18 stands); §4.7.9, the CH-MSGSOCK Preconditions bullet, and §29.2 26a/26b are retimed to match — BECAUSE the tree cancels the pod MCP surface at runtime-idle and re-arms it with that start's fresh manifest nonce on every solo claim (pkg/adapter/slotsession.go claimPodMCPStartLocked, cancelPodMCPIfRuntimeIdle; pkg/adapter/manifest.go writeSessionManifest mints per write), so a process-held connection from session 1 is to a retired surface on a recycling pod; the problem statement (verified) names exactly this defect, and 0084's impacts row already assumes one MCP connection per session — ALTERNATIVES: once-per-process dial (finding's option A) rejected: it re-creates the stated defect and survives only through the gap 0084 records (accepted MCP connections outlive a cancel because ServeConn runs on context.Background, pkg/adapter/mcp/server.go ServeConn), serving session 2 on session 1's connector set.

Pre-existing, owned by 0084 (which keeps the phrase, gate-pinned). EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go claimPodMCPStartLocked/cancelPodMCPIfRuntimeIdle; spec/04_system-components.md mcpNonce row

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: The staged spec edits add only intra-pod frames (CH-MSGSOCK session_start/session_end, sessionId on CH-RUNTIMEOPS, the Part B nonce line). They add no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write, no watch, and no work queue, so Postgres failover and Redis reset leave them unaffected. On the shipped sidecar path the coordinator hold timeout still ends the connection through CloseListener before the per-session closes run, which is what session_end rule 1 describes. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:189-250
FACT: Each start rewrites the manifest with a fresh mcpNonce, while the running intra-pod MCP servers keep validating the nonce they were armed with (mcpArmedNonce). Reading the nonce per session therefore fails for a second concurrent session until 0084 pins the nonce. The summary already records this as a 0084 dependency, so it is not a finding here. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/manifest.go:284; pkg/adapter/platformmcp.go:46; spec/04_system-components.md:844
WATCHOUT: The rule-8 rollback and a reclaim Shutdown can both write session_end for one session. The per-slot guard serializes a successor attempt behind the rollback, and session_end rule 3 absorbs the duplicate, so no successor's session_start is undone. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/resume.go:52-60; spec/04_system-components.md:725-731
OPEN: No spec rule says what a runtime does with a session-scoped CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame that names a session it does not hold. CODE-4 item 9 logs and ignores it, and that frame can overtake session_start because the two travel on separate connections. The adapter's deadlines bound the consequence (interrupt -> INTERRUPT_TIMEOUT, checkpoint_ready timeout), so I filed nothing. A correctness lens could check whether credentials_acknowledged has a stated timeout.

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: InProcessRuntime (embedded) WriteEnvelope writes an unbuffered io.Pipe, and Close already blocks on <-done until the loop returns, so an embedded session_end write before Close adds no stall beyond the pre-existing one. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/embedded.go:93-108, 186-203
FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess.WriteEnvelope is a bare conn.Write with no deadline; sidecar Close returns at once, so a session_end write on Shutdown is the only new socket write on the teardown path. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:455-468
DECISION: did not file the sidecar write-deadline stall on Shutdown — BECAUSE it needs a full socket send buffer and the proposal records write deadlines as a deliberate non-goal (summary.md:86) — ALTERNATIVES: filing under (e) rejected as speculative.
FACT: StartSession sets st.started at claim time and writes nothing to the runtime between Runtime.Start and noteRuntimeStarted; the rule-8 rollback catches a reclaim that raced ahead of session_start ONLY when session_start is written before the rule-8 confirmation. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:172-185, 323-326
WATCHOUT: the pod MCP surface is cancelled at occupancy zero (cancelPodMCPIfRuntimeIdle) and re-armed by a later claim with a fresh nonce, so "a later session uses the connections opened on the first session" is false for MCP. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:385-395; spec/04_system-components.md:919

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
FACT: The platform MCP server is armed once with the nonce of the start that took the once-per-pod claim and is stopped only when a release leaves the runtime serving no session, so a sequential later session gets a freshly armed server; a concurrent later session's dial with the current manifest nonce is rejected at the handshake. SPEC-5's "dials MCP sockets once per session after session_start" therefore works sequentially and fails closed concurrently, which the summary already attributes to 0084. DECISION: the post-session_end llm_request_completed exception lives once, in SPEC-3 **Inbound: `session_end`** rule 2; CODE-4 item 4 cites it and narrows its drop to CH-MSGSOCK writes; the duplicate edge-case bullet ("A handler goroutine that the author leaked can write after OnTerminate") is deleted — BECAUSE the adapter clears the per-provider in-flight counter only on CH-RUNTIMEOPS close (pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go close path, `for provider, n := range lc.inflight`), which under 0079 outlives sessions, so a barred completion wedges proactive_renewal rotations without bound (spec/04 §4.7 in-flight gate, ceiling exempts proactive_renewal) — ALTERNATIVES: make llm_request_completed process-scoped again (loses direct-mode token attribution, reverses summary item 15); adapter-side decrement on session_end (adapter cannot tell which in-flight requests belong to the ended session, because llm_request_started carries no sessionId); add sessionId to llm_request_started (new wire field, larger than a one-clause exception).

### [spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-3 **Inbound: `session_end`** rule 1 now writes the rule-8 rollback's session_end only when the adapter then holds no entry for the slot identifier; a rollback that finds an entry (a successor attempt's, different token) writes none — BECAUSE frames carry only sessionId, so a superseded attempt's session_end would release the successor's context, which rule 3 made the successor depend on — ALTERNATIVES: a token field on session_start/session_end (new wire surface; registry already distinguishes the arms); runtime-side per-session counting (a reclaim's session_end plus the rollback's still releases the successor); session_start after confirmation (reopens the round-1 reclaim race); deleting the rollback session_end (leaks a context when no successor exists); noteRuntimeStarted returning the arm (signature change on three callers).
WATCHOUT: Do not require the rollback's session_end to be written under s.mu or "within the rule-8 start step": WriteEnvelope blocks (embedded io.Pipe, socket transport with no write deadline), so that freezes the registry lock. CODE-1 item 2 re-reads with slotStateLocked under s.mu and writes after releasing it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/embedded.go:93-108, pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:455-466
WATCHOUT: The successor-entry arm of rollbackUnconfirmedStart still calls s.Runtime.Close; on the embedded transport that Close hazard predates 0090 and was left untouched as a separate finding. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimegeneration.go:105-112

### [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: session_end rule 1 narrows the rule-8 rollback arm to "the adapter then holds no entry for the session's slot identifier"; a rollback finding an entry (a different token) writes no session_end — BECAUSE the frames carry only sessionId, so that entry's attempt owns the runtime's context for the session — ALTERNATIVES: a bind-attempt token on session_start/session_end (new wire field plus per-generation runtime state in every SDK; rejected as new protocol surface); reference-counting starts/ends in the runtime (fails when the reclaim's session_end lands after attempt 1's session_start); writing session_start only after confirmation (reopens the reclaim race round 1 closed); deleting the rollback session_end (leaks a context per raced start).
DECISION: rollbackUnconfirmedStart re-reads the registry under s.mu (slotStateLocked) and does not need noteRuntimeStarted to return which arm refused — BECAUSE the later read is the one closest to the write: a successor admitted in between is seen and suppresses the write, and a successor gone in between correctly gets the release. No signature change for the three noteRuntimeStarted callers.
WATCHOUT: do not write the session_end while holding s.mu ("within the start step"). EmbeddedRuntime WriteEnvelope writes an unbuffered io.Pipe, and socket WriteEnvelope is a conn.Write with no deadline, so a frame write under the registry lock stalls every adapter RPC. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/embedded.go:93-108; pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:455-466
WATCHOUT: a residual interleave remains: a successor admitted AND started (session_start written) between rollbackUnconfirmedStart's re-read and its write. That needs a full admission RPC chain to fit in a gap of microseconds. Do not answer it with a lock held across I/O or a new hold. Record it here rather than staging it.
CORRECTS [spec.1.review-performance.1]: "The per-slot guard serializes a successor attempt behind the rollback" is false for StartSession. StartSession takes no per-slot guard between Runtime.Start and noteRuntimeStarted, so a successor's PrepareWorkspace(T2) and StartSession can run while attempt 1 waits in Runtime.Start for the runtime's first dial. Only Resume holds the guard for its whole call. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:124-185; pkg/adapter/resume.go:52-60
FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess.Close(ctx, sessionID) is a no-op, so on the sidecar the rollback's only effect on the runtime is the frame the proposal adds. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:533-535
OPEN: on the embedded transport, rollbackUnconfirmedStart's existing Runtime.Close(ctx, S) runs even when a successor of the same session owns the entry. InProcessRuntime binds one session per loop, so that Close may tear down the successor's loop. This hazard predates 0090 and is a tree finding for a later round. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimegeneration.go:105-111; pkg/adapter/embedded.go:63-88

EVIDENCE: proposals/0079_*/0079_*.spec-changes.md:535; grep "Annotated|protocol trace" over proposals/0090_* returns nothing.
OPEN: the docs mirrors (docs/reference/adapter-contract.md "Complete Basic-level session trace", docs/tutorials/build-a-runtime.md "Protocol Trace") still open on `message` and end on `shutdown`; DOCS-1 items 1 and 6 do not name them. DECISION: filed it as a design-defect whose fix is a reduction: restrict the rollback's session_end to the no-entry arm, written inside the start step. BECAUSE it closes the race without adding an attempt field to the wire. ALTERNATIVES: carry bind_attempt on the frames. Rejected because it adds a wire field and the runtime would have to compare tokens.

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-3 Edit 12 bounds every wait for the slot serialization by the deadline of the holder's own work (request deadline for Shutdown, DemoteSDK, and start; the existing ten-second close window for the §10.1 hold-timeout termination, cited and not restated) — BECAUSE it matches every lockSlotGuard caller (pkg/adapter/bindattempt.go lockSlotGuard(ctx); pkg/adapter/holdstate.go guardCtx with heldSessionCloseWindow = 10s) and adds no constant — ALTERNATIVES: "the bound this paragraph states for the cleanup's close" (leaves DemoteSDK, releaseClaimedSlot, and SIGTERM demotion unbounded); a new named constant or IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE (non-spec constant needs an override; makes leaked-versus-released untestable).
DECISION: DemoteSDK is a slot-serialization holder from its session_end decision until it answers, named once in Edit 12's holder sentence; both DemoteSDK rows of **Session frame writes.** point at it ("made under the slot serialization", "The decision the row above makes") — BECAUSE the decision precedes the §5.2 cleanup, so "a cleanup that reclaims the slot" never covered it, and both the not-running and running decisions can be overtaken by an open sequence — ALTERNATIVES: "until it removes the entry" (no end when the runtime close fails and no entry is removed); registry critical section (does not exclude an open sequence, which writes outside s.mu).
WATCHOUT: Do not stage a rule for a DemoteSDK whose slot-serialization wait expires. It expires only after the gateway's 5s DemoteSDK deadline, and §4.7 then fails and replaces the pod, so the stream ends with the pod. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md ConfigureWorkspace/DemoteSDK rows in the §4.7.1 RPC table
WATCHOUT: CODE-1 item 3 (non-spec-changes.md, DemoteSDK handler) already holds lockSlotGuard across the running read, the session_end write, and releaseSessionSlotUnderGuard; it needs no edit for the DemoteSDK holder clause. Moving the session_end write after sw.DemoteSDK is a separate open question and was not staged.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: slot-serialization wait bound = the deadline bounding the holder's own work (request deadline; §6.1 SIGTERM demotion timeout), and for the hold-timeout termination the graceful window §5.2 already states for its close — BECAUSE it matches every lockSlotGuard caller and adds no new constant — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "the bound this paragraph states for the cleanup's close" rejected: that sentence covers only a reclaiming Shutdown and the hold-timeout termination, so DemoteSDK's cleanup and a start's failure handler (releaseClaimedSlot) would stay unbounded; a new named constant rejected (no spec basis, would need operator override).
DECISION: DemoteSDK is named as a slot-serialization holder from its session_end decision until it answers, in Edit 12's holder sentence (the one home); the two DemoteSDK table rows only point at it — BECAUSE the demotion's decision and runtime close precede its §5.2 cleanup (§5.2 table row "deregisters nothing and runs no cleanup"), so "a cleanup that reclaims the slot" does not cover the decision.
FACT: lockSlotGuard(ctx) gives up at ctx expiry but takes a free guard even on an expired ctx; Shutdown, releaseClaimedSlot, releaseSessionSlot and DemoteSDK use the request ctx, the hold-timeout pass uses one heldSessionCloseWindow (10s) guardCtx shared by all members — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/bindattempt.go lockSlotGuard; pkg/adapter/holdstate.go guardCtx; pkg/adapter/session.go Shutdown removing arm; pkg/adapter/slotsession.go releaseClaimedSlot/releaseSessionSlot.
WATCHOUT: an unguarded DemoteSDK (guard wait expired) can still mis-decide running vs not running; this is not specified because the wait only expires past the gateway's 5s DemoteSDK deadline, which §4.7 maps to pod `failed` and replacement, so the runtime's stream ends with the pod — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md ConfigureWorkspace row "If DemoteSDK also fails, the pod transitions to failed"; ShutdownDemoteSDK (pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go) runs only at SIGTERM, before pod exit.
OPEN: DemoteSDK writes session_end before sw.DemoteSDK; when that close fails the entry stands (§5.2 table row) and the session continues on a runtime that has released it. Not this group's finding; a later round should decide whether the session_end write moves after a successful close.

### [spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The staged spec edits (SPEC-1 to SPEC-8) touch no CRD status or spec field, no finalizer, no admission webhook, and no controller reconcile. Every new mechanism (open sequence, slot serialization, rule-8 entry-identity confirmation, nonce handshake) is adapter in-process state or intra-pod wire, so the Kubernetes-idiom lens has no surface here beyond the existing `Runtime.spec.requireSoPeercred` and `sessionPolicy.maxConcurrentSessions` references, both of which exist. EVIDENCE: pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/runtime_types.go:71; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:396; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:478 (reclaim hold is adapter-held, refused on ABORTED, no report or counter)

### [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: In the staged Session frame writes table, only the Shutdown rows tie the session_end decision to the registry critical section; the DemoteSDK rows decide "while the entry stands", and the decision, the write, the SDK teardown, and the removal are separate acts. The §5.2 slot serialization (SPEC-3 Edit 12) names only a reclaiming cleanup and a start's open sequence as holders, so nothing in the spec excludes a ConfigureWorkspace open sequence recording `running` between DemoteSDK's decision and its removal. CODE-1 item 3 does take lockSlotGuard before the read, so the code closes the window the spec leaves open. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:262-263, :317; non-spec-changes.md:38-42; spec/04_system-components.md:673-674, :718
FACT: "the bound on that wait" in SPEC-3 Edit 12 has no antecedent in §5.2; the only bound the paragraph states is on the cleanup's close of the session. The code's bound is the request ctx (lockSlotGuard). EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:317; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:478; pkg/adapter/bindattempt.go:231-239
WATCHOUT: Shutdown and open-sequence session_end writes are mutually exclusive (Shutdown writes only on a recorded session, the open sequence only on a refused second confirmation, and both decide under the registry lock), so do not file a double-write there. DemoteSDK is the only end row whose decision sits outside that lock.
USEFUL [spec.4.review-mechanism.1]: its FACTs on the Addressing paragraph and the trace saved re-deriving both, and neither needed refiling.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: The proposal adds no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write; every new write is an intra-pod JSONL frame, so the top-tier control-plane write-rate math is zero delta. The performance surface is the adapter's blocking writes under the slot guard. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit 5 table; pkg/adapter/bindattempt.go:231 (lockSlotGuard)
FACT: Embedded-model RuntimeProcess writes over an unbuffered io.Pipe, so WriteEnvelope blocks until the runtime loop reads its next frame; a synchronous loop mid-turn does not read. WriteEnvelope takes no context on any transport. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/embedded.go:73-74,93-108; pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:455-466
WATCHOUT: §5.2 bounds only "the cleanup's close of the session on the pod's shared runtime process"; SPEC-1 Edit 6 places the session_end write before that close, so as staged the write has no bound. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:478; spec-changes.md:78
FACT: The pod-wide in-flight counter gating credentials_rotated is pre-existing and already acknowledged in spec, so it is not a finding against this proposal. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:891
FACT: Code already counts an unguarded cleanup as not completed with the hold kept, so SPEC-3 Edit 12's "act without slot serialization is a failed act" codifies shipped behavior and does not regress it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:299-302; pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:276-288

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The staged §5.2 slot-serialization text (SPEC-3 Edit 12) names "the bound on that wait" for a cleanup's wait, and no spec or staged text defines it; the only bounds in the §5.2 hold paragraph are on the cleanup's close of the session (Shutdown graceful window, request deadline, or ten seconds for the hold-timeout termination, which runs under no request). FACT: Every failed-act row in the §5.2 disposition table holds the identifier for the life of the pod, so a cleanup that bypasses the serialization cannot let a successor start; the "hold lasts at least until the open sequence ends" claim is sound given a bound exists. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:464-474
DECISION: did not file the DemoteSDK decide-then-remove interleave against a concurrent ConfigureWorkspace open sequence — BECAUSE rule 3 absorbs the repeat session_start and a later Shutdown or pod end releases the context; CODE-1 takes the slot guard in DemoteSDK anyway — ALTERNATIVES: filing as a violation of the "every session_start is followed by session_end" sentence, rejected as benign.
DECISION: did not file session_start/session_end write stalls (no write deadline) — BECAUSE summary non-goals list write deadlines explicitly (summary.md "Ending the shared connection on any write error, or adding write deadlines").

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
DECISION: filed no finding on `llm_request_completed` gaining a runtime-supplied `sessionId` (SPEC-4 Edit 3) — BECAUSE direct-mode token counts are already a runtime self-report accepted as residual risk and restricted to single-tenant or development deployments (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:49); per-session attribution by the same untrusted runtime does not widen a cross-tenant bound — ALTERNATIVES: filing as a trust-boundary regression; rejected as merely less strict.
FACT: SPEC-3 Edit 12's slot serialization fails closed: an act performed without the serialization counts as a failed act, which lands on the §5.2 table rows that hold the identifier for the life of the pod or enter `leaked` — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:466-472
FACT: the adapter's CH-MSGSOCK listener accepts exactly one connection per pod (pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go acceptLoop), while CH-RUNTIMEOPS re-accepts after each disconnect (pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go Run). SPEC-8's "the adapter keeps accepting" after a failed handshake changes the CH-MSGSOCK accept path in code (CODE-7), not the threat model: the nonce has never defended against a process that can read the manifest.
FACT: `session_start` carries only `credentialsPath`, a name, and `llm.headers` (no proxy URL or token), so §4.7.11 item 4 "No credential material over socket" still holds after SPEC-3 — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1002; proposal spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit 5 table

### [spec.3.fix-G1.1]
MISTAKE: SPEC-3 Edit 7 replaced the §28.5.3 "exits zero" completion sentence with "When `error` is absent, the `response` completes the session's task successfully." That made every error-free response a terminal completion, while sessions answer many messages and complete only through terminate, delete, or expiry. Edit 7 now deletes the sentence with no replacement.
WATCHOUT: do not restore any per-response, per-turn, or per-session success sentence in the **Error reporting via `response`.** paragraph. A session is one execution (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md "each session has exactly one execution"), a runtime is available again after emitting output (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md ready_for_input), and the gateway has no response-driven terminal path (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/lifecycle.go emitTerminalLifecycle). A "turn succeeds" sentence lost because nothing defines or consumes a turn. — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:680
OPEN: the paragraph's "When `error` is present, the adapter maps the task to `failed`" sentence has the same per-response-terminal problem and may conflict with SPEC-3 **Session errors.**; left unedited this round by design.
FACT: the Standing context already carried the settled owner decisions and the `*spec-changes.md` glob trap when this round started; no edit to it was needed. — EVIDENCE: review-log.md ## Standing context

### [spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]

EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:314; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:478 (ten-second graceful window).

### [spec.5.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The r3-prefix to r5 diff touches only SPEC-3 Edit 7 (sentence deletion) and SPEC-7 Edit 4 (the §29.4 steps 13/14 swap). No spec text cites §29.4 step 13 or 14 by number, and the §29.4 intro's "steps 10 through 18" still holds after the swap. EVIDENCE: grep "29.4" spec/*.md; spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:592
FACT: The open-sequence and Shutdown session_end decisions cannot both fire for one in-flight start. Shutdown removes the entry under the registry critical section, which the slot serialization does not block. It then finds the session not `running` and writes nothing, and the start's second confirmation is refused and writes the one session_end. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit 5 table rows "second confirmation is refused" and "Shutdown that removes an entry whose session did not reach running"
WATCHOUT: SPEC-1 Edit 6 (Shutdown row: "writes session_end when the session reached running") and the Session frame writes Shutdown row ("removes an entry whose session reached running") use slightly different conjuncts. They differ only for a repeated Shutdown that removes no entry, and runtime rule 3 makes a duplicate session_end harmless. Edit 6 also cites the table, so it was not filed. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:78, :260-261

### [spec.5.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The adapter sets no write deadline on any CH-MSGSOCK or CH-RUNTIMEOPS write (no SetWriteDeadline under pkg/adapter). A session_end write to a runtime that stopped reading is therefore unbounded on the adapter side. The bound comes from the gateway (the Shutdown deadline, then the §5.2 leaked or hold-for-pod-life rows) and from DemoteSDK failure making the pod `failed`. This predates the proposal for message and heartbeat writes. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go subscriber/send; spec/04_system-components.md:673 ConfigureWorkspace row.
FACT: In the CODE-1 DemoteSDK handler, the per-slot cleanup runs under the slot guard that DemoteSDK already holds (releaseSessionSlotUnderGuard). Read the SPEC-3 Edit 12 "one holder at a time" sentence as covering DemoteSDK and its own cleanup as a single holder, which rules out self-deadlock. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 3.
WATCHOUT: The SPEC-8 redial rule ("dials again when the adapter closes the connection before that frame") sets no backoff and no limit. Leave it unfiled: the loop is local to the pod, touches no store or apiserver, and is bounded by the rate of manifest rewrites. Do not file it as a stampede.
DECISION: No findings this round under the reliability lens. BECAUSE: the open sequence plus the §5.2 slot serialization keeps session_end ordered before a later attempt's session_start. Every wait for the serialization is bounded, and an act that times out falls to a failed-act row that is held for the pod's life. The hold-timeout rows end the connection or loop. Rule 3 of session_start and session_end makes duplicate frames idempotent. ALTERNATIVES: filing an unhandled-session case for session-scoped CH-RUNTIMEOPS frames addressed to a session the runtime does not hold. Rejected because the reply timeouts that already exist (interrupt, a 60 s credentials_acknowledged timeout, checkpoint) bound it, and the race needs the runtime to handle the two sockets out of order.

### [spec.8.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: the SPEC-5 Handler comment and the TaskID comment say OnCreate runs "once per session", but SPEC-3 session_start rule 1 writes a session_start for each start and each resume, and a same-pod retry after reclaim puts a second session_start for one sessionId on the same runtime process after its session_end (rule 3 only suppresses a duplicate while the session is held). BECAUSE the cardinality is a contract an SDK test asserts — ALTERNATIVES: not filed: DemoteSDK wait-overrun outcome (a DemoteSDK that cannot decide fails, and §4.7.1 sends a failed DemoteSDK to pod `failed`); the §8.8 TaskID citation (settled, see WATCHOUT at review-log ~867); Full sample's checkpoint_complete assert under interleaved session frames (illustrative, pre-existing).
FACT: the r6->r8 diff only retargets the taskId-equality citation to `CreateRequest.TaskID` in §15.7 and drops it from the session_start sessionId row; both verified against spec/15_external-api-surface.md:2229-2236 — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:102, :227
FACT: Resume is "Restore from checkpoint on a replacement pod" only, so the Resume row of Session frame writes never re-opens a session on the pod that already holds it — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:684
WATCHOUT: non-spec CODE-4 item 7 and the Python/TS docstrings (non-spec-changes.md:80, :96) copy "once per session"; if the Handler comment changes, the fixer propagates them. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:80

### [spec.8.review-performance.1]
FACT: The proposal writes nothing to etcd, Postgres, or Redis. Every new write is an intra-pod JSONL frame (session_start or session_end, about one per session start or teardown) or a per-start manifest rewrite, which already exists. The slot serialization is per slot identifier, so it puts no single lock in front of sessions on different slots. Under this lens the store-budget math is empty. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit 5 and Edit 12
FACT: The embedded-model InProcessRuntime carries frames over an unbuffered io.Pipe, so a session_end write blocks until the runtime loop reads. The current Close already waits without a bound on the loop's done channel, so a Shutdown session_end write on a busy or hung embedded loop stalls no worse than the current Close does. The finding was refuted by this comparison and should not be re-raised. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/embedded.go:93-110, pkg/adapter/embedded.go:188-205
FACT: A direct-mode llm_request_completed that arrives after the Shutdown flush loses its tokens in the current single-sink code (pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:405-416) as well. The SPEC-4 "drops them when the pod holds no binding" rule therefore does not lower budget accounting below the current behavior. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:405

### [spec.10.review-performance.1]
FACT: The proposal adds no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write. Every new write is an intra-pod CH-MSGSOCK frame (one session_start per start or resume, at most one session_end per teardown) plus in-memory rule-8 confirmations, so the top-tier write-rate math is zero against every store budget. Postgres failover and Redis reset do not reach any staged mechanism. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit 5 **Session frame writes.** table
FACT: The coordinator handoff path writes no session frame. A new coordinator exits hold through CoordinatorFence and re-attaches; it issues no StartSession, and Resume runs only on a replacement pod, so the runtime keeps its session context across a handoff. Hold timeout in the sidecar model closes CH-MSGSOCK first, so any open sequence holding the §5.2 slot serialization fails its write quickly rather than consuming the 10 s wait bound once per session. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:57-58; spec/04_system-components.md:684 (`Resume` restores "on a replacement pod")
FACT: Late direct-mode tokens (an llm_request_completed after Shutdown removed the binding) are dropped under both the shipped soleSession sink and the staged "drops them when the pod holds no binding" rule. Accounting is no worse than shipped, and the in-flight rotation counter is still decremented by the unchanged sentence in that row. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/usage.go:211-224; spec/28_communication-channels.md:1088
WATCHOUT: The pod-wide per-provider in-flight gate for credentials_rotated predates this proposal. Once rotation becomes session-addressed on concurrent pods, other sessions' traffic can delay one session's rotation. This is pre-existing and bounded by the 60 s rotation timeout fallback, so it is not a finding against 0090. — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:1088; spec/04_system-components.md:896

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-7 item 3 makes `SocketRuntimeProcess.acceptLoop` the handshake retry loop in every SocketPeerAuth mode — BECAUSE `wrap` returns the bare listener when NonceOnly or NoSocketBoundary is set, so no keep-accepting path exists in nonce-only mode, the one mode where the handshake is the only authentication — ALTERNATIVES: handshake inside wrap's check closure (never runs in nonce-only, cannot hand the bufio.Reader on); always installing peerCheckedListener (changes the wrap decision for the MCP listeners too).
WATCHOUT: acceptLoop today accepts exactly one connection for the pod's life; a handshake added without a loop ends acceptance on the first refused dial, and the runtime's redial after a replaced nonce is never accepted. Only the nonce-only test posture discriminates this — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go acceptLoop; pkg/adapter/peercred.go SocketPeerAuth.wrap and peerCheckedListener.Accept.
FACT: the in-tree code that plays the adapter side of CH-RUNTIMEOPS accepts rather than dials: cmd/lenny-compliance/full.go newFakeAdapter/handshake, sdks/runtime/go/runtime/lifecycle_test.go fakeLifecycleAdapter, tests/tier10_conformance/credential_path_resolution_conformance_test.go fake. cmd/lenny-compliance dials only the MCP probe (standard.go net.Dial). No unit-test files exist under sdks/runtime/python or sdks/runtime/typescript — EVIDENCE: find sdks/runtime/{python,typescript} -name '*test*' outside node_modules returns nothing.
FACT: in nonce-only mode the MCP server writes `_lennyChallenge` in place of the initialize response (pkg/adapter/mcp/server.go, RequireChallenge branch before initializeResponse); no SDK MCP client answers it. An unanswered challenge line decodes as an empty successful response, so only an assertion on a later `tools/list` discriminates — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/mcp/server.go handleConn; pkg/adapter/platformmcp.go and connectormcp.go `srv.RequireChallenge = s.PeerAuth.NonceOnly`.
DECISION: SDK MCP clients answer the challenge in CODE-7 item 5 (finding option a) — BECAUSE problem-statement scope item 5 and SPEC-8 Edit 6 assign it here, and 0084 owns only the server side — ALTERNATIVES: defer to 0084 and narrow Edit 6 (contradicts the problem statement's scope, reopens spec text).
DECISION: the Part B tests moved from TEST-1 into a CODE-7 "Tests:" paragraph so S19 lands them with the code; S18 tiers unchanged — ALTERNATIVES: reorder S19 before S18 (moves every TEST-1 case behind CODE-7 for no reason).
DECISION: decision 25 and the spec-changes edge bullet are cut to pointers at SPEC-8 Edit 1 **Runtime connection handshake.**; decision 25 keeps the case list as the reasoning for depending on no 0087 ordering or 0084 pinning. The edge-bullet edit touches spec-changes.md (Edge cases section only; no staged spec text changed).
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md]: SPEC-8 Edit 1 **Runtime connection handshake.** does not state that the adapter refuses a connection when no manifest has been published (empty provider value). The deleted decision-25 sentence was its only prose statement; it now lives only in CODE-7 item 1 ("refuses an empty provider value") and the CODE-7 tier-1 test. Add "A connection accepted while no manifest is published is refused." to Edit 1.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: `writeSessionStart` gates `credentialsPath` on the registry entry's `slotState.assigned` (read under `s.mu`) — BECAUSE `assignCredentialsSlot` is the only writer of the credential file and the only setter of `assigned`, and the marker is never cleared while the entry lives — ALTERNATIVES: stat the file at write time (I/O in the open sequence, races credfile's rename); SDK tolerates a missing file (contradicts the staged field row and CODE-4 item 6's fail-closed create error).
FACT: `sessionCredentialsPath` returns the slot path whenever a credentials root is configured, whether or not a file was written, so the old "resolves to the empty string" predicate never omitted the member under the default `--credentials-dir` — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/manifest.go sessionCredentialsPath; pkg/adapter/slotcreds.go assignCredentialsSlot (sets `st.assigned = true`); pkg/adapter/slot.go slotState.assigned.
FACT: All three SDK `CredentialBundle` types decode flat `mode`/`apiKey`/`apiKeyEnv`/`baseUrl`, while credfile.Write emits `{"providers":[{leaseId, provider, expiresAt, deliveryMode, materializedConfig}]}` (§4.7.11 item 4), so today's decoded bundle is empty. CODE-4 item 8 now restates the bundle as a `Providers` list with `MaterializedConfig` left undecoded; CODE-5 item 3 points at it — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/types.go CredentialBundle; pkg/adapter/credfile/credfile.go credentialDocument; spec/04_system-components.md §4.7.11 item 4 "Fields per entry".
WATCHOUT: Do not type `MaterializedConfig` per delivery mode or add flat ProxyURL/LeaseToken fields — the direct-mode object is provider-specific under §4.9 and the file holds one entry per provider — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md `materializedConfig` Schema by Provider.
WATCHOUT: The tier-10 Go no-file test `TestGoRuntimeSDKStartsWithNoCredentialFileAtTheManifestPath_spec_4_7` does not match the `…FromTheManifest` pattern; once the manifest fallback is gone it passes vacuously unless it sends a `session_start` without `credentialsPath`. CODE-4 item 12 now names it — EVIDENCE: tests/tier10_conformance/credential_path_resolution_conformance_test.go.
DECISION: CODE-4 item 12 keeps the `…FromTheManifest` → `…FromSessionStart` rename in a parenthesis beside the design's colon list — BECAUSE the design's replacement dropped the rename of `TestGoRuntimeSDKResolvesCredentialPathFromTheManifest_spec_4_7`, and CODE-5 item 8 still names `…FromSessionStart` for Python and TypeScript.
OPEN: The doc comment on `sessionCredentialsPath` (pkg/adapter/manifest.go) names the manifest field and the "manifest's statement" for an empty root; CODE-8 makes it stale and no deliverable names it. A later round decides whether CODE-8 should name it.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: The harness opens a session for a CH-RUNTIMEOPS send only after it has read the `response` to a `message` it wrote after that session's `session_start` (CODE-3 item 4) — BECAUSE the Go SDK reads CH-RUNTIMEOPS on its own goroutine (lifecycle.go `go lc.loop`), so write order across stdin and the socket orders nothing — ALTERNATIVES: buffering unknown-session events in the SDK (new mechanism, conflicts with session_end rule 3); a heartbeat_ack barrier (does not prove that OnCreate finished).
DECISION: The compliance `session_start` prefix helper moved from CODE-6 item 1 into CODE-3 item 4 (S11). CODE-6 item 1 is now a pointer and keeps its number because other groups cite CODE-6 items by number — BECAUSE CODE-4/CODE-5 answer prefix-less messages with RUNTIME_ERROR, which turned S14/S15 red until S16, while pre-change SDKs ignore unknown frame types (runtime.go default case).
WATCHOUT: EOF and `shutdown` must DRAIN queued messages before OnTerminate. Only `session_end` discards them. Reusing the session_end teardown for EOF breaks every Basic compliance check, because driveAdapter closes stdin right after it writes — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-compliance/main.go driveAdapter `defer stdin.Close()`; sdks/runtime/go/runtime/runtime.go Run drains `messages` before invokeTerminate.
DECISION: The frame loop removes a session from its routing table when it reads `session_end`. A later `session_start` for the same id creates a new state whose OnCreate waits for the removed state's OnTerminate (CODE-4 item 4) — BECAUSE a same-pod retry (rollbackUnconfirmedStart -> Aborted) otherwise hits the stale entry as a duplicate and its message gets RUNTIME_ERROR — ALTERNATIVES: a timeout on the predecessor wait (lets OnTerminate run out of order); an exception clause in item 2 (unnecessary once the entry is removed); a synchronous teardown in the loop (violates item 1).
FACT: Neither the Python nor the TypeScript runtime SDK has a unit-test suite or runner. The established pattern is Go-driven subprocess tests with inline probe scripts (tests/tier10_conformance/credential_path_resolution_conformance_test.go, tests/tier3_contract/sdks/runtime_sdk_test.go). TEST-1's Python/TypeScript row is now tier 3 in runtime_sdk_test.go — EVIDENCE: no test_*.py or *.test.ts under sdks/runtime; sdks/runtime/typescript/package.json has no test script.
DECISION: The two-session credential-isolation cases are a new TEST-1 tier-10 row in credential_path_resolution_conformance_test.go, not a tier-9 row — BECAUSE that file holds the only per-SDK credential-bundle probes and credRotationAdapter, and test helpers cannot be imported across test packages.

DECISION: reject tests for session_lifetime and the rewritten checkDeadlineSignal live in CODE-6 items 2 and 3 (tier-1, stub runtimes, sessionecho_test.go pattern), not TEST-1 — BECAUSE TEST-1 carries cross-component tests — ALTERNATIVES: one reject stub per row obligation (restates the row)
OPEN: other Basic checks (response_echoes_session_id, schema checks) are also missing from checkLevel; pre-existing, not staged here.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SDK MCP clients (Go connectMCP, Python mcp.py, TS mcp.ts) answer the nonce-only challenge in CODE-7 item 5 (option a) — BECAUSE problem-statement scope item 5 assigns "the SDKs answer ... challenge ... on CH-MSGSOCK, the intra-pod MCP servers, and CH-RUNTIMEOPS" here, the platform and connector MCP servers already challenge in NonceOnly, and SPEC-8 Edit 6 already states it — ALTERNATIVES: deferring to 0084 (contradicts the problem statement; leaves every SDK nonce-only runtime unable to complete MCP initialize).
DECISION: CODE-7 item 3 makes SocketRuntimeProcess.acceptLoop loop on handshake failure in every SocketPeerAuth mode — BECAUSE wrap returns the bare listener in NonceOnly/NoSocketBoundary, so peerCheckedListener's retry loop is absent exactly where the handshake is the only authentication — ALTERNATIVES: handshake inside the wrap check closure (never runs in nonce-only mode, cannot hand the bufio.Reader on); always installing peerCheckedListener with a nil peer check (changes wrap's single decision for every listener, larger).
DECISION: Part B tests move from TEST-1 into CODE-7 so S19 lands them; Python/TS client-half coverage is one tier-3 case per SDK with a test-owned CH-RUNTIMEOPS listener (close-once on a replaced nonce, then challenge) plus a RequireChallenge platform MCP server — BECAUSE Python and TS have no in-tree unit-test suites, and adding a challenge or redial to the lenny-compliance fakes would change battery semantics SPEC-6 does not state — ALTERNATIVES: a lenny-compliance nonce-only flag (new harness mechanism).
FACT: the Python and TypeScript runtime SDKs have no unit-test files in-tree (find sdks/runtime/{python,typescript} -name '*test*' returns nothing); they are exercised only through tests/tier3_contract/sdks/runtime_sdk_test.go running lenny-compliance. TEST-1's row "Each SDK's unit-test suite" for Python/TS names a suite that does not exist (separate finding, not filed here).
FACT: CH-MSGSOCK socket dials in the SDKs are runtime.go:823 (Go), runtime.py:614 (Python), runtime.ts:571 (TS); CODE-7's old targets named only {transport,lifecycle} for Python/TS. EVIDENCE: grep dial_unix_socket / dialUnixSocket.
FACT: the in-tree CH-RUNTIMEOPS accepters (fakes playing the adapter) are cmd/lenny-compliance/full.go newFakeAdapter/handshake, sdks/runtime/go/runtime/lifecycle_test.go, and tests/tier10_conformance/credential_path_resolution_conformance_test.go; tests/testinfra/rotationgate DialPeer is a dialer. cmd/lenny-compliance dials only the MCP probe (standard.go:589).
WATCHOUT: today an SDK MCP client that receives `{"_lennyChallenge":...}` decodes it as an rpcResponse with nil Result and nil Error, so `call("initialize")` returns success and the failure surfaces only at tools/list. A test asserting initialize succeeded does not discriminate; assert tools/list arrives after a valid challenge answer. EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/mcp.go connectMCP/call.
WATCHOUT: verify the SDK lifecycle examples dial the platform MCP socket when the manifest names one; if not, the tier-3 MCP half runs against the delegate example instead.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]

FACT: `sessionCredentialsPath` returns the slot path whenever a credentials root is configured (default `/run/lenny` from `--credentials-dir`), regardless of whether a file exists. Only `assignCredentialsSlot` writes the file and sets `slotState.assigned`; the gateway skips AssignCredentials when the bind names no pool or user provider, and Resume mints no lease. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/manifest.go:336-343; pkg/adapter/slotcreds.go:26-53; pkg/adapter/slot.go:38-49; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/credentiallease.go:89-93; pkg/adapter/resume.go:259
DECISION: the `session_start` builder sets `credentialsPath` only when the session's registry entry records `assigned` — BECAUSE that marker is the existing fail-closed was-assigned predicate the rotate and revoke handlers already use, and AssignCredentials precedes every start row, so no new state is needed — ALTERNATIVES: stat the file at write time (races the atomic rename, adds I/O to the open sequence); tolerate a missing file in the SDK (contradicts the staged field row and CODE-4 item 6's fail-closed create error).
FACT: the SDKs' `CredentialBundle` (Go types.go, Python types.py, TS types.ts) decodes flat `mode`/`apiKey`/`apiKeyEnv`/`baseUrl` keys, while `credfile.Write` writes `{"providers":[{leaseId, provider, expiresAt, deliveryMode, materializedConfig}]}` per §4.7.11 item 4. Every decoded field is empty today. The spec defines no CredentialBundle fields, so this is an SDK defect rather than a spec one. — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/types.go:96-116; pkg/adapter/credfile/credfile.go:34,59-75; spec/04_system-components.md:1030-1044
DECISION: fix the bundle layout in CODE-4 item 8 (CODE-5 points at it) rather than file a RECORDS-1 gap — BECAUSE CODE-4 already rewrites the same loader, the proposal deletes `llm.baseUrl` so the file is the only proxy-URL carrier, and the staged §15.7 Edit 14 promises the per-session API key — ALTERNATIVES: RECORDS-1 finding (leaves staged spec text false at landing); restoring `llm.baseUrl` on session_start (spec-loop matter, and the lease token stays unreachable anyway).
WATCHOUT: `TestGoRuntimeSDKStartsWithNoCredentialFileAtTheManifestPath_spec_4_7` does not match CODE-4 item 12's `…FromTheManifest` pattern; once the manifest fallback is deleted it sends no `session_start`, so OnCreate never runs and its nil-bundle assertion passes vacuously. Name it explicitly. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier10_conformance/credential_path_resolution_conformance_test.go:247
WATCHOUT: `sdks/runtime/go/runtime/mcp_test.go:308` asserts the flat `Provider`/`APIKey` bundle fields; it is already on CODE-4 item 12's update list and must move to the providers layout.
OPEN: the `sessionCredentialsPath` doc comment (pkg/adapter/manifest.go:326-335) describes "the manifest's credentialsPath" and the empty-root case as "the manifest's statement"; CODE-8 makes that stale and no deliverable names the comment. Separate finding for a later round.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: the compliance harness's session opening (the `session_start` prefix helper for main.go, standard.go, and full.go, and the rule that full.go writes a CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame naming the session only after reading the `response` to a `message` for it) has one home, CODE-3 item 4; CODE-6 item 1 becomes a pointer to it, and CODE-4 item 12 cites it for the rotation retarget — BECAUSE S11 precedes S14/S15, and pre-change SDKs and reference runtimes ignore an unknown frame type, so the prefix can land first and S14/S15 stay green — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it in CODE-6/S16 (S14 and S15 red until S16); putting it in CODE-4/S14 (S15 does not depend on S14); buffering unknown-session CH-RUNTIMEOPS events in the SDK (new mechanism for a harness-only ordering gap).
WATCHOUT: write order across CH-MSGSOCK and CH-RUNTIMEOPS orders nothing in the SDK: the lifecycle loop is its own goroutine started inside startChannels before the stdin frame loop. Only a read `response` proves the frame loop has entered the session in its routing table. — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/lifecycle.go:146; sdks/runtime/go/runtime/runtime.go:243-283
OPEN: the same cross-socket race exists in production: an `interrupt_request` or `credentials_rotated` the adapter writes before the SDK's frame loop has read `session_start` is dropped under CODE-4 item 9 (an interrupt then times out to INTERRUPT_TIMEOUT, and a rotation goes unacknowledged). The spec states no ordering between the two channels. This is a spec question for a later round; nothing is staged here.
DECISION: SDK EOF and `shutdown` drain each live session's queue (queued messages dispatch, then OnTerminate per session); only `session_end` discards queued messages (CODE-4 item 5; CODE-5 item 3 points at it) — BECAUSE driveAdapter closes stdin right after writing (cmd/lenny-compliance/main.go:304-321), the current SDK drains before OnTerminate (runtime.go:283-290), and the CH-MSGSOCK `shutdown` contract says "finish current work and exit".
DECISION: `session_end` removes the session from the frame loop's routing table when the loop reads it; a later `session_start` for that id creates a new state whose OnCreate waits for the removed state's OnTerminate to return — BECAUSE a same-pod retry writes session_end(S) then session_start(S) on one connection (rollbackUnconfirmedStart answers codes.Aborted, pkg/adapter/runtimegeneration.go:105-112), and deleting the state asynchronously made the retry's start look like a duplicate — ALTERNATIVES: running the new OnCreate without waiting (OnTerminate(S) could then run after OnCreate(S) and destroy a handler's state keyed by sessionId); a handler that ignores cancellation stalls only that retry's dispatch, which is the handler's own defect.
DECISION: cross-session credential isolation is pinned at tier 10 in tests/tier10_conformance/credential_path_resolution_conformance_test.go, one case per SDK, and tier 9 is not added to S14/S15 — BECAUSE that file holds the only fixtures that load SDK credential bundles (the inline Python/TS probe scripts and credRotationAdapter), and no tier-9 test imports a runtime SDK — ALTERNATIVES: a tier-9 file (duplicates the probe fixtures across packages, since test helpers cannot be imported).
FACT: neither the Python nor the TypeScript runtime SDK has a unit-test suite or a test runner. The only executable tests of either are Go tests that run it as a subprocess: tests/tier3_contract/sdks/runtime_sdk_test.go (examples) and tests/tier10_conformance/credential_path_resolution_conformance_test.go (inline probe scripts such as pythonCredProbe). — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/typescript/package.json scripts; tests/tier10_conformance/credential_path_resolution_conformance_test.go:586-624

FACT: `lenny-ctl runtime validate` level reconciliation keys on check names in `checkLevel` and silently skips unmapped names, so renaming a compliance check without editing that map removes it from the verdict. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-ctl/runtimescaffold/probe.go:47-55, :124-127, :144-147

This is a pre-existing defect outside this proposal's scope. Do not fold it into CODE-6. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-compliance/main.go:220-233 against probe.go:47-67
DECISION: the session_lifetime frame sequence (including "read A's response before A's session_end") has one home: the SPEC-6 Edit 1 row. CODE-6 item 2 already points to it. — BECAUSE the row is the normative statement and the CODE item cites it — ALTERNATIVES: appending the read step to CODE-6 item 2 (the reviewer's fix) restates the sequence in a second place and leaves the spec row stating an unread sequence
DECISION: delete the TEST-1 "share one fixture sequence" sentence rather than move the fixture to pkg/compliance. — BECAUSE cmd/lenny-compliance is package main (main.go:22), the tier-10 case needs a non-null experiment variant the compliance fixture does not carry, and CODE-6 item 6 already runs session_lifetime against every SDK example at tier 3. An exported test fixture in pkg/compliance would add API surface only for a test. — ALTERNATIVES: pkg/compliance export (the reviewer's fix), rejected as surface added only to serve tests

### [non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: the adapter writes credentials.json only in assignCredentialsSlot, and the gateway skips AssignCredentials for a session with no pools or user providers; sessionCredentialsPath returns the path whenever CredentialsDir is set. A frame field built from sessionCredentialsPath therefore names a missing file for every leaseless session. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotcreds.go:26-53; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/credentiallease.go:89-93; pkg/adapter/manifest.go:336-343
FACT: SocketPeerAuth.wrap returns the raw listener in nonce-only and embedded modes, so peerCheckedListener's refuse-and-keep-accepting loop exists only in default mode; SocketRuntimeProcess.acceptLoop accepts exactly one connection. RuntimeOps.Run loops on its own. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/peercred.go:32-50,135-140; pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:331-352; pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:213-238
FACT: the compliance Full battery, the Go SDK lifecycle_test, and the tier-10 credential_path_resolution test each run a fake CH-RUNTIMEOPS SERVER that the SDK dials; none is a dialer. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-compliance/full.go:126-176; sdks/runtime/go/runtime/lifecycle_test.go:17-60; tests/tier10_conformance/credential_path_resolution_conformance_test.go:294-330
FACT: driveAdapter writes every input line and closes stdin at once; the Go SDK lifecycle loop runs on its own goroutine started before the stdin loop. Any SDK teardown that discards queued work on EOF, or drops CH-RUNTIMEOPS events for a session not yet opened, races the compliance harness. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-compliance/main.go:304-321; sdks/runtime/go/runtime/lifecycle.go:146; sdks/runtime/go/runtime/runtime.go:236-290
WATCHOUT: the proto comments in schemas/lenny-adapter.proto for StartSessionRequest, ResumeRequest, and ConfigureWorkspaceRequest still say that experiment and tracing context reach the runtime in the manifest, and that the adapter derives the manifest taskId. SCHEMA-1 item 6 touches only RunSetupRequest. — EVIDENCE: schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:945-957,1376-1393,1735-1737
CORRECTS [review-log line 1075 OPEN, CredentialBundle]: no SDK reads the manifest llm.baseUrl, so deleting it changes nothing for the SDKs. The flat CredentialBundle versus the materializedConfig layout is a pre-existing mismatch that this proposal does not worsen. — EVIDENCE: grep baseUrl in sdks/runtime finds only CredentialBundle parsers

### [non-spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: the proposal adds no etcd, Postgres, or Redis writes; every new write is an intra-pod frame on CH-MSGSOCK/CH-RUNTIMEOPS, so control-plane write-rate math is nil. The token sink's bound-session check takes s.mu once per llm_request_completed, the same lock soleSession takes today — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:421-430, pkg/adapter/runtimegeneration.go:154-169
FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess.acceptLoop calls Accept exactly once and installs the result; the only keep-accepting loop is inside peerCheckedListener.Accept, which SocketPeerAuth.wrap does not install in NonceOnly or NoSocketBoundary mode — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:331-350, pkg/adapter/peercred.go:32-50, :135-140
FACT: RuntimeOps.Run already loops (serveConn error -> resetConn -> Accept), so a CODE-7 handshake failure there keeps accepting with no structural change — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:212-235
WATCHOUT: under decision 25 a refused-then-redialed CH-MSGSOCK connection is a normal path (manifest rewritten by step 6 or a concurrent start), so the CH-MSGSOCK accept loop must retry after a failed handshake in every auth mode — EVIDENCE: summary.md decision 25; spec-changes.md:24
FACT: late llm_request_completed after teardown is dropped both by the shipped soleSession resolver (runtimeLive cleared) and by the staged bound-session check, so CODE-3's drop rule is no regression for the token budget — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimegeneration.go:161-169
FACT: no SDK or reference runtime emits llm_request_completed today; only tests and the adapter do, so S11 (CODE-3) landing before S14/S15 cannot drop production tokens — EVIDENCE: grep of sdks/ cmd/ for llm_request_completed

### [non-spec.1.review-plan-conformance.1]

DECISION: filed one plan-conformance finding: no deliverable makes the SDK MCP clients (Go mcp.go connectMCP/call, Python mcp.py, TS mcp.ts) answer the `_lennyChallenge` the intra-pod MCP servers send in nonce-only mode — BECAUSE plan 10.2 Phase 2 assigns "the runtime side of ... the intra-pod MCP servers (finding 4)" and problem-statement scope item 5 says SDKs answer the challenge on MCP; CODE-7 item 5 covers only CH-MSGSOCK and CH-RUNTIMEOPS dials, and SPEC-8 Edit 6 makes §15.7 say SDKs "answer the nonce-only challenge" — ALTERNATIVES: treating it as covered by decision 18 (rejected: 18 declines per-session redial/nonce lifetime, not challenge answering) or by the server-first non-goal (rejected: that declines a server change and names the CH-MSGSOCK/RUNTIMEOPS order-tolerant client).
CORRECTS [review-log 575, 724]: those plan-conformance passes folded the MCP challenge answer into "per-generation nonce on intra-pod MCP declined by decision 18"; the challenge answer is a separate runtime-side deliverable no decision covers.
FACT: MCP servers issue the challenge after reading initialize and before the initialize response when PeerAuth.NonceOnly — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/mcp/server.go:183-187, pkg/adapter/platformmcp.go:31, pkg/adapter/connectormcp.go:84. No SDK source contains "challenge" — EVIDENCE: grep over sdks/runtime/{go/runtime,python/lenny_runtime,typescript/src}.

### [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess.acceptLoop accepts exactly one connection and returns; the keep-accepting refusal loop lives only in peerCheckedListener.Accept, which SocketPeerAuth.wrap installs only when neither NonceOnly nor NoSocketBoundary is set. In nonce-only mode logRefusedPeer is never called. RuntimeOps.Run, by contrast, loops on Accept and survives a failed serveConn. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:331-351; pkg/adapter/peercred.go:135-140, :32-50; pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:213-238
WATCHOUT: CODE-7 item 3 says a failed CH-MSGSOCK handshake "follows the existing refuse, log, and keep-accepting path of logRefusedPeer"; that path does not exist in nonce-only mode, the mode the challenge is for. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:119
WATCHOUT: same-pod retry after a refused second confirmation writes session_end(S) then a later attempt's session_start(S) back to back on one connection; CODE-4 deletes the session state only at the end of the asynchronous teardown and ignores a duplicate session_start, so the retry's start is dropped. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:75, :77; spec-changes.md:253 (closing guarantee names a later attempt's session_start)
FACT: no SDK emits llm_request_completed itself (grep over sdks/ and cmd/), so CODE-3 landing before the SDK steps does not silently drop SDK-emitted counts.
DECISION: did not refile the unbounded session_start/session_end write (WriteEnvelope has no write deadline) — BECAUSE the history refuted the session_end variant as hardening and the summary records the WriteEnvelope hazard — ALTERNATIVES: filing the session_start-under-guard variant, rejected as a close variant.

### [non-spec.1.review-security.1]
FACT: SocketPeerAuth.wrap returns the bare listener in nonce-only and embedded modes, so the "refuse, log, keep accepting" path (peerCheckedListener + logRefusedPeer) does not exist there; SocketRuntimeProcess.acceptLoop accepts exactly one connection and installs it. Any CH-MSGSOCK handshake placed "after the peer check" on that path is skipped in nonce-only mode. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/peercred.go:135-140; pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:331-350
FACT: RuntimeOps.Run already loops Accept -> serveConn -> resetConn, so a handshake failure in serveConn does keep accepting on CH-RUNTIMEOPS in every mode. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:213-237
WATCHOUT: RuntimeOps.serveConn binds lc.conn/lc.enc before any handshake, and CompleteCheckpoint/SignalDeadlineApproaching/SignalFilesUpdated write without waiting on ready; a pre-auth peer can receive one-way frames during the handshake window. Judged below the bar (metadata only, bounded by 500 ms), not filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:241-257, :476-543, :629-640
FACT: Direct-mode token counts are an accepted self-report residual risk restricted to single-tenant deployments (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:49), so CODE-3 keying the sink by the runtime-supplied sessionId is not a security regression.
DECISION: filed a missing-test finding for cross-session credential isolation in the SDKs — BECAUSE the tier-10 case defers credentials to credential_path_resolution, whose cases are all single-session — ALTERNATIVES: treat as optional; rejected because cross-user credential crossing is the proposal's headline security defect.

FACT: TypeScript minimal and coding templates compile unchanged after CODE-5 (`onTerminate(): void {}` and `onCredentialsRotated() {}` stay assignable); only typescript-chat's header comment names the manifest credentialsPath — EVIDENCE: templates/typescript-chat.main.ts.tmpl:13.
FACT: go-coding and python-coding also pass credentials-rotated callbacks (go-coding.main.go.tmpl:100, python-coding.main.py.tmpl:117), covered by "adopt the new ... lifecycle callback signatures".
DECISION: CODE-7 tier-3 per-SDK case extended to a test-owned CH-MSGSOCK listener named by LENNY_ADAPTER_SOCKET, with the `response` on the redialed connection as the discriminating assertion — BECAUSE no SDK test sets LENNY_ADAPTER_SOCKET (Go runtime_test.go uses WithSocketTransport(false)), and the Python/TS CH-MSGSOCK dials are separate sites (runtime.py:612-614, runtime.ts:571) from the lifecycle dial — ALTERNATIVES: separate CH-MSGSOCK case (duplicates fixture); Go-only tier-1 test (misses Python/TS).
WATCHOUT: a successful dial or accept on CH-MSGSOCK does not discriminate; the listener closes nonce-less connections silently, so only a `response` to a `message` proves the handshake passed.

### [non-spec.4.fix-G1.1]
MISTAKE: CODE-4 item 4 claimed "with this ordering the SDK writes no CH-MSGSOCK frame after session_end". False: cancelling the handler's context makes handleMessage write a RUNTIME_ERROR response with the sessionId, and ToolCall writes its tool_call before it selects on ctx. Replaced with a per-sessionState ended mark set by the frame loop under the stdout writer's lock and checked by every response and tool_call write. EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/runtime.go handleMessage (OnMessage error branch); sdks/runtime/go/runtime/tool.go AdapterTools.ToolCall.
WATCHOUT: do not key the post-session_end drop on sessionId or on routing-table absence. After the same-pod retry rule, a session_start for the same sessionId creates a new state whose answers must go out; the TEST-1 Go row's retry case now asserts "dispatches and answers" to catch a sessionId-keyed drop.
WATCHOUT: Tools/_tools/tools in the SDKs is the process-scoped dialed platform and connector MCP connection set (Go startChannels, python runtime.py PlatformTools.dial, ts runtime.ts Tools.dial), not a per-session helper. Never list it in a per-session record or retire it as a per-process field; per-session MCP belongs to 0084.
FACT: Python and TypeScript expose a public process-wide credential-path fallback, RunOptions.credentials_path (runtime.py RunOptions, _resolve_credentials_path) and RunOptions.credentialsPath (runtime.ts, `this.manifest?.credentialsPath || this.cfg.credentialsPath`). ### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-4 item 4's "With this ordering the SDK writes no CH-MSGSOCK frame ... logged and dropped" is replaced by a write gate: the frame loop marks the removed sessionState ended, under the stdout writer's lock, when it reads session_end; every response and tool_call written for that state checks the mark under the same lock and is logged and dropped, and a dropped tool_call returns an error at once — BECAUSE ordering cannot deliver SPEC-3 **Inbound: `session_end`** rule 2: cancellation makes the handler return an error, handleMessage answers it with a RUNTIME_ERROR response, and ToolCall writes its frame before it checks ctx — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "every write for the session after session_end is dropped" keyed by sessionId (rejected: the round-1 same-pod retry creates a new state for the same sessionId whose answers must not be dropped); suppressing only the error response when ctx is cancelled (rejected: a handler can still return a success reply or call ToolCall after the read).
FACT: handleMessage writes a RUNTIME_ERROR response for every OnMessage error, carrying env.SessionID — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/runtime.go:466-478
FACT: AdapterTools.ToolCall registers and writes the tool_call frame before selecting on ctx.Done — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/tool.go:44-54
WATCHOUT: the ended mark must be per sessionState, never per sessionId; TEST-1's retry case asserting the retried message is answered is what discriminates a sessionId-keyed drop — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 4 retry sentence, TEST-1 Go SDK row
FACT: Go's process struct is named `session` and holds the process-scoped `tools *Tools` dialed once in startChannels; CODE-4 items 2 and 10 calling it "the session's Tools" contradicted item 6 the same way CODE-5 items 1-2 did — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/runtime.go:170, :328-332; context.go:51

### [non-spec.4.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed two findings: (1) CODE-4 item 4's session_end ordering waits for the in-flight handler, whose return makes handleMessage write a `response` (and whose ToolCall writes a `tool_call` without checking ctx), so "the SDK writes no CH-MSGSOCK frame for the session after its session_end" does not follow; only post-OnTerminate writes are dropped. (2) CODE-7 never says where the pkg/runtimekit helper finds the manifest, and the test-only SocketRuntimeProcess.spawn sets only LENNY_ADAPTER_SOCKET while the tier-4 SpawnPath tests configure no ManifestDir, so at S19 the adapter refuses every CH-MSGSOCK connection (empty nonce) and the spawned echo runtimes poll /run/lenny forever — BECAUSE both are mechanisms that fail as staged, with tests in the proposal's own tier list that go red — ALTERNATIVES: filing the CH-RUNTIMEOPS-before-session_start production race (left as the existing OPEN; consequences are bounded timeouts), filing streaming-echo's own dialLifecycleSocket as an unstaged CODE-7 site (item 5 prose already names "the reference runtimes").
FACT: Go SDK handleMessage writes a RUNTIME_ERROR `response` whenever OnMessage returns an error, including ctx cancellation, and ToolCall writes its `tool_call` before selecting on ctx — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/runtime.go:466-478; sdks/runtime/go/runtime/tool.go:40-54
FACT: writeSessionManifest is a no-op when ManifestDir is empty, so no nonce is published; tests/tier4_integration/runtime_socket_lifetime_test.go, concurrent_workspace_test.go, and concurrent_delegation_proxy_test.go set SpawnPath with no ManifestDir — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/manifest.go:280-283; pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:437-440
FACT: streaming-echo, elicitation-echo, and delegation-echo dial CH-RUNTIMEOPS/MCP with their own net.Dialer helpers rather than pkg/runtimekit — EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/streaming-echo/main.go:256-283; cmd/runtimes/delegation-echo/main.go:508-525
FACT: SDK-warm ConfigureWorkspace runs on the in-process SDKWarmRuntime, so CODE-7's socket handshake never applies to it — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go:213-270

### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]

FACT: The proposal creates no control-plane or datastore writes. Every new write is intra-pod: two CH-MSGSOCK frames per session, a `sessionId` member on existing CH-RUNTIMEOPS frames, and one nonce line (plus a challenge in nonce-only mode) per runtime socket connection. No etcd, Postgres, or Redis budget is touched, so the capacity math at the top tier is zero new writes. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 to CODE-8; pkg/adapter/session.go:138-188 (StartSession path has no store write added)
FACT: `writeSessionManifest` mints a fresh `mcpNonce` on every call (StartSession, Resume, and ConfigureWorkspace), and CODE-7's nonce provider reads the currently published one. A runtime dial that races a concurrent start's manifest rewrite is refused and then converges through the SPEC-8 re-read and redial. This is not a livelock, because the rewrites are bounded by the start rate. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/manifest.go:280-301
FACT: `SocketRuntimeProcess.Close` is a no-op, so CODE-1's `s.Runtime.Close` on an open-sequence error does not tear down other sessions on a concurrent pod. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:533
FACT: CODE-3 item 5's bound-session token sink keeps counting during a coordinator hold, because the registry entry stays bound until hold-timeout pass 1. On a concurrent pod this is strictly better than the shipped `soleSession` resolver, which returns "" whenever the cohort is not 1. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:421-437; pkg/adapter/runtimegeneration.go:161-169
WATCHOUT: `SessionUsageMeter.sessions` never deletes an entry. Under multi-session reuse, an entry accumulates per session for the pod's lifetime. This predates the proposal and the proposal does not stage a fix. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/usage.go:116-131

### [non-spec.4.review-security.1]
DECISION: returned no findings under the security lens — BECAUSE CODE-7 fails closed (empty nonce provider refused, constant-time compare, challenge only in nonce-only mode, embedded model uses in-process runtimes with no socket), and the direct-mode token sink's trust in the runtime-reported sessionId is the spec's accepted residual risk (§11.2 direct mode is single-tenant or development only) that SPEC-4 stages explicitly — ALTERNATIVES: filing the pre-auth encoder binding in RuntimeOps.serveConn (rejected, see below).

### [non-spec.5.fix-G1.1]

The env/default rule is stated once in item 5 only for runtimekit.Open and binary-minimal, which have no option.

WATCHOUT: do not "fix" this by having the SDK export its resolved path into LENNY_ADAPTER_MANIFEST, or by re-implementing option-then-env-then-default inside runtimekit; both duplicate the SDK resolvers (and the env export races in parallel tests). CODE-7 item 7's t.Setenv stays correct because SpawnPath children start through runtimekit.Open.

### [non-spec.5.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 Go row pins CODE-4 item 4's tool_call drop with a late `ToolCall` using `context.Background()` from a goroutine A's handler left running, issued after A's `OnTerminate` returns, asserting no frame and an error well inside the timeout — BECAUSE AdapterTools.ToolCall writes the frame and then returns on ctx.Done() or the timer (default 30 s), so a call on the session's cancelled ctx, or an assertion without a time bound, passes ungated code; OnTerminate is the sync point that guarantees the mark is set — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer wording "handler or goroutine ... after session_end" (cancelled ctx does not discriminate, no sync point); a new TEST-1 row (second statement of the rule). EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/tool.go AdapterTools.ToolCall.
FACT: Standing context already carried the settled owner decisions and the *spec-changes.md glob trap; no edit needed.

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the CODE-7 item 5 runtimekit handshake helper takes the manifest path as an argument; each caller supplies the path it already resolves (Go SDK config.manifestPath via WithManifestPath, Python/TS RunOptions manifest path, in-tree tests the file they wrote), and only runtimekit.Open and binary-minimal, which have no option, use LENNY_ADAPTER_MANIFEST else /run/lenny/adapter-manifest.json — BECAUSE the precedence rule already lives in each SDK's options and the helper owning a second copy is what diverged — ALTERNATIVES: helper re-reads env/default and SDK sets env var (mutates process env, hair); helper takes an option struct (more surface than a string).
CORRECTS [review-log 1461]: the manifest path rule does not live in CODE-7 item 5's helper any more; the helper is path-parameterised and the env/default rule applies only to runtimekit.Open and binary-minimal.

FACT: the Go SDK imports only the standard library (no pkg/ import today); CODE-7 item 5 makes it import pkg/runtimekit — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/runtime.go:60-73, dialUnixSocket at :837 duplicates runtimekit.DialSocket
UNVERIFIED: whether making the stdlib-only Go SDK import pkg/runtimekit is acceptable (module/vendoring for external runtime authors); a later lens should check — not this finding.

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: close the missing post-session_end ToolCall test with one clause appended to the existing "no `response` or `tool_call` for A follows A's `session_end`" case in the TEST-1 Go SDK row: a goroutine A's handler left running calls `ToolCall` with `context.Background()` after A's `OnTerminate` returns, and the call writes no frame and returns an error well inside its timeout — BECAUSE this is the only scenario that separates the drop gate and the immediate error from the ungated behavior. CODE-4 item 4 already states the rule, so it is not restated. — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's wording "A's handler or a goroutine it started ... issues after session_end" was rejected because a handler still in flight would make `OnTerminate` wait, and a cancelled session ctx makes the early return non-discriminating; a new TEST-1 row was rejected (a clause is a lower rung).
WATCHOUT: the case discriminates only if the late `ToolCall` uses a context that is NOT derived from the session ctx. `ToolCall` writes the `tool_call` before it selects on ctx and returns early on a cancelled ctx, so with a cancelled ctx the immediate return comes from ctx rather than from the drop. The return must also be bounded far below the `AdapterTools` timeout (default 30 s), or the ungated code passes once the timer fires. — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/tool.go:40-65
WATCHOUT: sync on A's `OnTerminate` returning before releasing the leaked goroutine. CODE-4 item 4 marks the state when the loop reads `session_end`, and `OnTerminate` runs after the handoff, so the order is deterministic. An implementation that marks after the handoff fails this case, which is the intended result.
