### [non-spec.7.review-performance.1]
FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess.Close(ctx, sessionID) is a no-op, and WriteEnvelope is a bare conn.Write with no write deadline (no SetWriteDeadline anywhere in pkg/adapter). So the shipped Shutdown teardown on a socket runtime never touches the connection, and CODE-1's session_end write is the first connection write inside the guarded, hold-open teardown. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:455-466, :533-535; pkg/adapter/session.go:301-347
WATCHOUT: InProcessRuntime.WriteEnvelope writes to an unbuffered io.Pipe, and its Close already waits on loopDone with no bound, so the embedded model was unbounded before this proposal. Only the sidecar socket path regresses. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/embedded.go:93-108, :188-204
FACT: The proposal stages no gateway, Postgres, Redis, or etcd writes. All new frames are intra-pod. Control-plane write-rate math is therefore nil for this proposal.
DECISION: Did not file the CODE-3 token-sink checkSessionBound change. BECAUSE the shipped soleSession already drops every fold once runtimeCohort != 1 or after noteRuntimeClosed, so the new bound check is no worse. ALTERNATIVES: filing the deregistration-to-emitFinalUsage window was rejected as marginal.

### [non-spec.7.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings for the reliability lens — BECAUSE the one recovery-path hazard that survives tracing (CODE-1 writeSessionEnd has no write deadline, so a teardown can stall on a runtime that stopped reading: SocketRuntimeProcess.WriteEnvelope does a bare conn.Write; SubprocessExecutor.WriteEnvelope blocks on a full stdin pipe ahead of the 10 s Close kill; InProcessRuntime uses an unbuffered io.Pipe) was already refuted in an earlier round as pre-existing or accepted (non-spec.r7.history.md:74-80) — ALTERNATIVES: refiling it with the dev-loop SubprocessExecutor variant; rejected because the refutation's reasoning (the §5.2 bound covers only the close, and the failed-act rows absorb an expired waiter) applies to it unchanged.
FACT: the CH-MSGSOCK listener accepts exactly one connection for the pod's life (acceptLoop returns after it installs one), and the ended state is sticky, so the SPEC-8 "redial when closed before the first protocol frame" path happens only during the handshake. After a passed handshake the adapter closes the connection only in CloseListener. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:324-352, :533-560
FACT: the Go SDK loads the manifest once, after openTransport and before startChannels. Under CODE-7, the socket openTransport waits for the manifest, so the later loadManifest finds it. — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/runtime.go:229-246, :558-580
WATCHOUT: RuntimeOps.serveConn replaces lc.ready before its handshake, and a waiter that captured the previous channel (from the constructor or resetConn) is never released. This is pre-existing. CODE-7's refuse-then-redial adds more replace cycles, so place the nonce check before serveConn binds lc.ready/lc.conn. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:241-257, :265-288, :554-595
UNVERIFIED: CODE-4 item 5 says Run returns "after the last one" of the live sessions. It does not say whether a session already handed off by session_end, with OnTerminate still running at EOF, is waited for. A WaitGroup over every session goroutine covers it, and no TEST-1 row pins it. A correctness lens could decide whether this needs a sentence.

### [non-spec.7.review-security.1]

FACT: In direct mode the token sink already trusts the runtime's llm_request_completed self-report; CODE-3 item 5 only moves session attribution from the adapter's soleSession to the frame's sessionId, gated by checkSessionBound. FACT: The §4.7.6 llm.apiKeyEnv row today says the runtime sets the env var "in its own process"; SPEC-3's session_start row tightens this to per-session client or subprocess env. This is a tightening rather than a regression. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:855
FACT: EmbeddedPeerAuth is used only by the in-process echo-embedded and preconnect-echo runtimes, so CODE-7's "every SocketPeerAuth mode" does not reach a socket in the embedded model. — EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:148, cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go:124

### [redesign.7.introspection-remedies.1]
DECISION: CODE-7's caller enumeration, spread across the Targets, item 5, item 7, and the Tests paragraph, is replaced by one **Client inventory.** table with 49 rows, one per in-tree program, function, or test helper that dials or accepts `CH-MSGSOCK` or `CH-RUNTIMEOPS`, plus the SDK MCP clients item 5 changes. Columns: file and function, socket, dial or accept, manifest-path source, and the test that fails if the row is not done. Item 5 keeps the rule that the helper takes the caller's resolved manifest path once and re-reads that file on each dial; item 7 keeps the fake and `SpawnPath` rules; the Tests paragraph points at the table's last column; the summary's CODE-7 index line now points at the table — BECAUSE the introspection pass of the redesign run, round 7, found CODE-7 re-repaired in four rounds, each time because a prose caller list dropped a dial site (streaming-echo `dialLifecycleSocket` most recently), and a table derived from the tree makes a missing row visible — ALTERNATIVES: another prose patch (the failure mode the introspection named); a grep-based gate (no harness lane for it in this proposal).
FACT: rows were derived from a tree search for `LENNY_ADAPTER_SOCKET`, `runtimekit.Open` and `DialSocket`, every `"unix"` dial, `SpawnPath`, `NewSocketRuntimeProcess`, `NewRuntimeOps`, `rotationgate.DialPeer`, `net.Listen("unix", ...)`, `AF_UNIX`, and the TypeScript `dialUnixSocket` (`sdks/runtime/typescript/src/transport.ts` reads as binary to grep and needs `-a`). Excluded after checking: the MCP fakes and MCP-only dials (`delegation-echo` and `elicitation-echo` `dialMCPSocket`, `git-credential-lenny`), the embedded TCP runtimes (`echo-embedded`, `preconnect-echo`), and stdin users (`mcp-reference`, `SubprocessExecutor`) — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-7 **Client inventory.** and the paragraph below it.
WATCHOUT: the tier-9 foreign-UID dials (`dialAndExpectRefusal`, the inline dial in `TestRuntimeOpsSocketRefusesForeignUIDPeer_spec_4_7_11`) fail no test if left unchanged; they must present the valid nonce or the refusal stops being attributable to the UID. `cred-shell-echo`, `delegation-echo`, `elicitation-echo`, and `streaming-echo`'s `Open` dial have no test that drives them over the socket (`TestCredentialFileDeliveryContract` is skipped); only `Open`'s own tests cover them. `binary-minimal` is reached only by tier-5 `TestRuntimePublishJourney`.
DECISION: the cross-channel session ordering is settled. SPEC-3 `session_start` rule 1 states the write order on the `CH-MSGSOCK` connection or loop only. SPEC-4 Edit 2 is the one home of the hold: a runtime that keeps per-session context holds a session-scoped `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` frame naming a session it does not hold until that session's `session_start` is read or a bounded wait of its choosing ends, then drops it without a reply, and the wait ends before the frame's `deadlineMs`. The SPEC-3 Timing bullet's "in order" sentence is scoped to the same connection or loop. The spec Edge cases record the residual (a dropped frame falls to the adapter's own bound; a frame sent just before a session ended can reach a later start of the same session within the wait). CODE-3 item 4 drops the harness's wait for a `response` before a `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` send and cites the rule; CODE-4 item 9 implements it with `WithSessionStartWait` and the default as IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE; CODE-5 item 5 names the Python and TypeScript options; CODE-6 item 1 and the CODE-4 item 12 and TEST-1 tier-10 rotation cases point to CODE-3 item 4 or the rule; TEST-1 gains a Go tier-1 case and a per-SDK tier-3 row that deliver the frame before `session_start`; DOCS-1 items 1 and 3 state the hold once in `adapter-contract.md` and the option in `runtime-sdk.md`; summary decision 28 records it; checklist S4 now depends on S3 — BECAUSE the introspection pass of the redesign run, round 7, found the ordering gap deferred three times: rule 1 promised `session_start` before "any other frame addressed to the session" while `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` is a separate connection read on a separate loop (CODE-3 item 4) and CODE-4 item 9 dropped events for an unknown session, so a checkpoint, interrupt, rotation, deadline, or upload signal that overtook its `session_start` was lost with no spec statement of the outcome — ALTERNATIVES: an acknowledgement for `session_start` (rejected by the owner decision of no ack frames); session frames on `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` (rejected by the owner decision that they stay on `CH-MSGSOCK`); keeping the harness wait (it hides the race from the battery, and the hold makes it unnecessary); placing the hold in SPEC-3 (the rule governs receipt of `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` frames, and the `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` card owns the session-scoped frame list, as the `CH-MSGSOCK` card owns **Session errors.** for its own frames).
FACT: checked SPEC-6 Edit 1 (**session lifetime**) against SPEC-3 `session_end` rule 2: the row reads each of A's and B's `response` before that session's `session_end`, and C and D receive no `session_end` before stdin closes, so no message is pending when a `session_end` releases its session; no contradiction. The non-spec Edge-cases bullets are unchanged since the spec loop converged (d7bb5256f) and agree with SPEC-3 **Session frame writes.**; the refused SDK-warm start bullet now names the adapter as the writer. The spec Edge-cases bullets were not edited by the non-spec loop.

### [spec.12.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The r11->r12 diff is SPEC-6 Edit 4 (new sentence after `Each higher level inherits every test category from the levels below it.`), the deadline row dropping its own `session_start` clause in favour of Edit 4, and overview/summary/non-spec pointers to Edit 4. OPEN: A `session_start` written on stdin does not order the runtime's reads of a CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame, and SPEC-4 Edit 2 bounds the hold wait only from above ("a bounded wait of the runtime's choosing", which ends before `deadlineMs`). A conforming runtime with a near-zero wait could therefore drop a `checkpoint_request` or `interrupt_request` that the Full rows require it to answer. The fixer accepted this deliberately: non-spec CODE item 4 says "no wait for a response first". This round did not file it because it is a close variant of the round-11 fixed finding. A human should decide whether the harness should read a `response` for the session before it sends the frame.

### [spec.13.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the battery's session precondition (SPEC-6 Edit 4, its one home) now has the harness read the `response` to a `message` for a session before sending a `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` frame for it — BECAUSE a response proves the runtime read that session's `session_start` (CH-MSGSOCK rule 1 orders it first), so the frame is handled for any hold length; write order on stdin does not order reads across two connections — ALTERNATIVES: a one-second floor on SPEC-4 Edit 2's wait (probabilistic, conflicts with "ends before deadlineMs" for short deadlines); unbounded hold (credentials_rotated/files_updated have no deadline); dropping the reply requirement from the Full checkpoint/interrupt rows (spec/15_external-api-surface.md §15.4.6 rows, guts the categories); a session_start ack frame (owner settled: none).
WATCHOUT: do not add a minimum to SPEC-4 Edit 2's "bounded wait of the runtime's choosing" and do not copy the precondition into the Full checkpoint, interrupt, or deadline rows; SPEC-6 Edit 4 is its one home. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-6 Edit 4 fenced block.
MISTAKE: round 11 gave the harness a write-order precondition (write session_start first) and the summary/CODE item 4 defended "no wait for a response"; a conforming zero-wait runtime whose CH-RUNTIMEOPS reader runs ahead of stdin then fails checkpoint/interrupt rows nondeterministically. Cost: round 13 finding.
CORRECTS [Standing context, Settled, cross-channel ordering entry]: its clause "the compliance harness does not wait for a `response` before a `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` send" is now false; the harness reads a `response` for the session first (SPEC-6 Edit 4). The hold rule itself (SPEC-4 Edit 2) is unchanged.
FACT: the review-log OPEN recording this read-order gap (near the ledger entry citing §15.4.6 checkpoint/interrupt rows) is closed by this edit.

### [spec.13.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the zero-wait conformance race in the harness's precondition (SPEC-6 Edit 4): before a session-scoped CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame the harness reads the `response` to a `message` for that session; SPEC-4 Edit 2's hold stays unchanged — BECAUSE a response for session X proves the runtime read X's session_start (CH-MSGSOCK rule 1 order), so the frame names a held session and is handled whatever wait the runtime chose; this is an observed-state precondition, a different kind from round 11's write-order precondition — ALTERNATIVES: a wait floor ("at least one second") in SPEC-4 Edit 2 is a degree-narrowing of the rule, still probabilistic under scheduling delay, and collides with "ends before deadlineMs" for a short deadline (needs a further exception clause); deleting the drop leaves credentials_rotated/files_updated with no bound.
CORRECTS [Standing context, Settled, cross-channel ordering entry]: its clause "the compliance harness does not wait for a `response` before a `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` send" was a loop decision (redesign round 7), not an owner answer; it is the cause of the round-13 finding. True after this fix: the harness reads a response for the session first (SPEC-6 Edit 4). The rest of that entry (hold home in SPEC-4 Edit 2, SDK wait as a Run option) still holds.
FACT: cmd/lenny-compliance/full.go checkCheckpointQuiesce (~:318) and checkInterruptAck (~:359) send the CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame with no prior message exchange, so CODE item 4's rewrite of those checks must add one. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-compliance/full.go:341,381
WATCHOUT: the Full deadline row (SPEC-6 Edit 2) writes deadline_approaching before reading its message's response; that stays valid because the Edit 4 precondition (an earlier message/response) precedes it. Do not edit the row. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-6 Edit 2

### [spec.13.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: kept the round-13 SPEC-6 Edit 4 precondition (harness reads a `response` to a `message` for the session before a `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` frame) and made the Full **deadline signal handling** row (SPEC-6 Edit 2) build on it: the row now sends a second `message` after that exchange, then `deadline_approaching` before reading the response, and forbids another `response` for the session only after `deadline_approaching` — BECAUSE the row is the one Full category whose `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` send must precede its message's response, and its old "no other `response` for that session" clause counted the precondition's own response — ALTERNATIVES: exempting the deadline row from Edit 4 (reopens the zero-wait race spec.13.fix-G1 closed); "no other `response` to that `message`" (responses carry no message correlator, so a harness cannot check it).
FACT: CODE-6 item 1 now cites SPEC-6 Edit 4 for the `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` send order, and item 3's check fails on a second `response` after `deadline_approaching` — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 items 1 and 3.
CORRECTS [spec.13.fix-design-G1 WATCHOUT "Do not edit the row"]: the row needed editing; without it the row's response count and CODE-6 item 3 failed every conforming runtime.
WATCHOUT: citer sweep over **deadline signal handling**, `checkDeadlineSignal`, "second `response`", and "that prefix" found no other falsified site; summary decision 16 ("requires no unsolicited `response`") stays true. Standing context's cross-channel ordering entry still says the harness does not wait for a `response`; spec.13.fix-G1 already filed the CORRECTS for compaction.

### [owner.2026-10-04]
DECISION: applied the owner decisions of 2026-10-04. (1) `session_start` is acknowledged by a runtime-written `session_started` on CH-MSGSOCK, and the adapter writes no session-scoped CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame for a session before it reads that acknowledgement; this replaces the SPEC-4 Edit 2 hold-and-drop rule, the SDK wait options (`WithSessionStartWait`, `session_start_wait_s`, `sessionStartWaitMs`), the tier-1 and tier-3 hold tests, the Edge-cases residual about dropped early frames, and the old summary decision 28. (2) `DemoteSDK` fails closed when its wait for the slot serialization outlasts its request's deadline — BECAUSE three convergence stops all traced to the cross-channel ordering race: a runtime-side hold with a runtime-chosen wait left the order between CH-MSGSOCK and CH-RUNTIMEOPS nondeterministic, so each harness precondition and wait bound the loop added moved the race rather than removing it. The acknowledgement makes the order deterministic, because the adapter's read of `session_started` happens after the runtime has created the session's state. The `DemoteSDK` answer was the one open decision the review loop could not derive — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the hold with a wait floor (still probabilistic, and it collides with short `deadlineMs`); moving the session frames to CH-RUNTIMEOPS (the owner placed them on CH-MSGSOCK); a `DemoteSDK` that proceeds without the slot serialization (the staged CODE-1 text before this decision, which the owner rejected).
DECISION: the adapter waits for `session_started` only when the runtime's CH-RUNTIMEOPS capability handshake completed before the open sequence began, and the runtimes that must write it are those that keep per-session context or that opened CH-RUNTIMEOPS — BECAUSE a Basic-level runtime that keeps no per-session context ignores `session_start` (SPEC-3 `session_start` rule 2) and would otherwise fail every start; the acknowledgement exists to order CH-RUNTIMEOPS, which such a runtime never opens — ALTERNATIVES: waiting on every runtime (breaks Basic runtimes and the SPEC-6 **session lifetime** Edge case); a capability declaration (owner rejected one on 2026-10-02).
DECISION: the wait sits inside the open sequence, between the `session_start` write and the second rule-8 confirmation, and the acknowledgement-failure row writes `session_end` before the start's error arm — BECAUSE the record (`running`) then implies the runtime holds the session, the slot serialization keeps a reclaim from interleaving with the wait, and the closing pairing guarantee of **Session frame writes.** still holds for a runtime that holds the session or a failed-create mark — ALTERNATIVES: waiting after the record (a reclaim could interleave, and a timed-out session would be `running`).
DECISION: the wait bound is an adapter flag `--session-start-ack-timeout` with an IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE default (CODE-9 item 2), below both the 10 s `ConfigureWorkspace` timeout and the ten-second §5.2 graceful window — BECAUSE the spec states its other intra-pod adapter timeouts as fixed values (500 ms handshake, 10 s heartbeat ack, 60 s rotation ack), so its convention does not require operator tuning, and code-best-practices requires a flag for a default the spec does not fix; the gateway's `StartSession` call carries no deadline, so the request deadline alone does not bound the wait — ALTERNATIVES: fixing a value in the spec (a new constant with no derivation).
DECISION: `session_end` stays unacknowledged — BECAUSE an acknowledgement would order nothing: after `session_end` the adapter writes no frame for the session on either channel, and a CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame written before it is ordered by neither connection's write order whether or not the runtime answers. The residual (a frame written before `session_end` and read after it) is in the spec Edge cases.
DECISION: added CODE-9 as its own step (S18) and renumbered TEST-1, CODE-7, and RECORDS-1 to S19, S20, and S21 — BECAUSE the SDKs need the adapter's `session_start` (CODE-1, S12) before they can serve sessions, while the adapter's wait needs every in-tree runtime that opens CH-RUNTIMEOPS to write `session_started` (CODE-4, CODE-5, and CODE-6); CODE-1 keeps only the Attach drop of `session_started` so an acknowledgement written between S14 and S18 reaches no client. The compliance harness keeps an interim response-first order from CODE-3 until CODE-6 switches it to the `session_started` read.
FACT: folded the conformance assertion into the existing Full **CH-RUNTIMEOPS opening** row (SPEC-6 Edit 5) rather than adding a category, so the §18.8 check counts ("full (12/12)") stay true. EVIDENCE: spec/18_build-sequence.md §18.8 exit criteria.
FACT: Python and TypeScript have no in-tree tier-1 runner (no test files under sdks/runtime/python or sdks/runtime/typescript/src), so their per-SDK `session_started` cases are the tier-3 probe subtests in tests/tier3_contract/sdks/runtime_sdk_test.go; Go has both tiers.
CORRECTS [Standing context, Settled, "Cross-channel session ordering" bullet]: replaced by the owner decision of 2026-10-04 above.
CORRECTS [reconcile.2 OPEN, summary.md decision 28]: closed; decision 28 now states the acknowledgement and cites SPEC-3 and SPEC-4 Edit 2.
CORRECTS [reconcile.2 OPEN, DOCS-1 item 5 testing.md precondition]: closed; DOCS-1 item 5 has `testing.md` state the SPEC-6 Edit 4 precondition and follow SPEC-6 Edits 1 to 3 and 5.
CORRECTS [reconcile.2 DECISION, summary Open decisions]: the `DemoteSDK` slot-serialization question is answered and removed from the summary's open decisions, which now record both owner answers.
FACT: the finding "Conformance precondition orders a message exchange before every CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame, including lifecycle_capabilities" is resolved: SPEC-6 Edit 4 now requires the `session_started` read only before a session-scoped CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame and states that a process-scoped frame such as `lifecycle_capabilities` has no precondition.
WATCHOUT: no site states the hold rule. A sweep of summary.md, spec-changes.md, non-spec-changes.md, and implementation-checklist.md for the hold, the SDK wait options, "no acknowledgement", and "neither frame" finds only the reclaim hold of §5.2, the coordinator hold timeout, and the `shutdown` and `files_updated` texts, which are unrelated.

DECISION: applied the owner-delegate fixes from the fresh review of commit ea644786e: (1) CODE-9 item 7 adds the `lenny-adapter --session-start-ack-timeout` row (basis no-spec-value) to tests/registers/deployment-boundary-defaults.yaml in the flag's commit; (2) SPEC-3 Edit 3 names which runtimes answer `session_start` and says "`session_start` and `session_end` each travel"; (3) SPEC-4 Edit 7 adds a `session_start` arm writing `session_started` to the §15.4.4 Full sample's main loop; (4) DOCS-1 item 5 adds the acknowledgement step to both Full-level step lists in integration-levels.md; (5) CODE-9 item 6 gives `InProcessRuntime.Output` and `SubprocessExecutor.Output` fan-out semantics so a cancelled wait subscription consumes nothing, with a tier-1 case per transport; (6) the summary CODE-1 index names attach.go; (7) a start that did not wait records the session as not awaiting an acknowledgement, so its CH-RUNTIMEOPS frames flow ungated, stated in SPEC-3 Edit 16 rule 3, the SPEC-4 Edit 2 gate, and CODE-9 item 1, with a tier-1 case — BECAUSE these are owner-delegate decisions closing gaps the review found: an unregistered non-spec default, a Timing sentence that overstated who acknowledges, a Full sample that never acknowledged, docs step lists missing the step, frame loss on two transports, and a pending record that blocked forever when no wait ran — ALTERNATIVES: none recorded; the coordinator supplied each decision.

### [spec.16.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `session_start` gains a required adapter-minted `startId` that `session_started` echoes; rule 5 drops any `session_started` whose `startId` is not that of the open sequence waiting for the session — BECAUSE `sessionId` is shared by every attempt of a session and a §5.2 slot retry can land on a new slot of the same pod, so a late acknowledgement of attempt A was read as attempt B's (or B's errored ack was ignored because B already held a record) — ALTERNATIVES: fail the pod on a missed ack (reopens the 2026-10-04 owner decision and kills co-tenants); per-session count of owed acks (rule 1 does not oblige an ack after `session_end`, so counts misattribute); echo the rule-8 bind token (empty token is shared, SPEC-3 Edit 13) or the slot id (not on the wire); optional `startId` (conditional, leaves executor and compliance frames unchecked by schema).
WATCHOUT: the old rule-5 clause "and that has no §4.7.1 rule-8 record" must not come back beside the `startId` match; it is what let an errored ack for a session that already held a record be silently ignored. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit 16 rule 5; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md §5.2 **Slot retry policy** ("The retry is always assigned to a **new slot** on the same pod").
FACT: writers that wait for no ack use a constant `startId` ("1" in the CODE-2 executor, "compliance-1" in the CODE-3 helper); the adapter mints from a per-`Server` atomic counter (CODE-1 item 1 step c). The discriminating test assertion is the earlier-`startId`-while-a-later-start-waits clause in TEST-1 `TestSessionStartedGatesRuntimeOpsAndBoundsTheStart_spec_28_5_3`.
UNVERIFIED: SCHEMA-1 item 3 fixtures `jsonl.session_start.json` and `jsonl.session_started*.json` are not described member by member, so they must carry `startId` to validate; the non-spec lane should confirm on its next pass.

### [spec.16.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: a late `session_started` of an earlier start of the same session is told apart by a required per-start `startId` that the adapter mints for each `session_start` and the runtime echoes; SPEC-3 Edit 16 rule 5 drops any `session_started` whose `startId` is not the waiting open sequence's — BECAUSE `sessionId` is shared by every attempt and a §5.2 slot retry lands on a new slot on the same pod, so slot serialization does not separate the attempts — ALTERNATIVES: fail the pod when the ack wait ends without the frame (rejected: reopens the 2026-10-04 owner decision that a missing ack fails the start, and kills co-tenant sessions on a concurrent pod); count owed acks per session and skip one per timed-out start (rejected: rule 1 does not force a runtime that reads `session_end` before finishing creation to ack, so the count misattributes); reuse the rule-8 bind attempt token (rejected: requests carrying no token share the empty token, SPEC-3 Edit 13).
WATCHOUT: the echo is of the `startId` of the `session_start` being answered, including a repeated one (Inbound `session_start` rule 3); an SDK that echoes the stored first frame's `startId` on a re-answer stalls the later start until its ack timeout. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 2 "A duplicate `session_start` is otherwise ignored and is answered with `session_started` again".
FACT: `session_start` frame literals that must gain `startId` live in spec-changes SPEC-3 Edit 5 example and Edit 11 trace, SPEC-4 Edit 7 Full sample (`session_started` literal), non-spec CODE-2 item 2 executor literal, CODE-3 item 4 compliance helper literal, and SCHEMA-1 item 2 `$defs`. §29 traces (SPEC-7) quote no frame JSON. EVIDENCE: grep '"session_start\|"session_started' over both changes files.

### [spec.16.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed two findings: (1) `session_started` carries only `sessionId`, so on a same-session retry on the same pod (§5.2 slot retry "new slot on the same pod") attempt A's late acknowledgement satisfies attempt B's open-sequence wait; B takes the record before the runtime has read B's `session_start`, and B's own `session_started{error}` then lands on a recorded session and is consumed with no effect, so the start-failure gate is bypassed; (2) the wait predicate is written out at SPEC-1 Edit 4 (§4.7.9 step 10), SPEC-3 Edit 16 rule 3, and SPEC-7 §29.2 step 31 with different conjuncts ("has completed" vs "completed before that open sequence began") — BECAUSE both change what a test must assert — ALTERNATIVES: filed nothing on the DemoteSDK deadline row's citation of §6.1 **Adapter SIGTERM behavior during `sdk_connecting`.**: the row names two callers (a gateway caller, and the SIGTERM path's internal DemoteSDK during sdk_connecting), so the citation fits the second.
FACT: the adapter's CH-RUNTIMEOPS handshake is asynchronous to StartSession (pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:213 Run/serveConn, :561 WaitHandshake; pkg/adapter/session.go:86 StartSession never waits on it), so whether the first pod-warm session is gated depends on dial timing; rule 3 accepts that. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:547-561
FACT: same-session retry on the same pod is reachable at maxConcurrentSessions > 1. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:598
WATCHOUT: at ConfigureWorkspace/DemoteSDK time a gateway-claimed pod is `claimed`, not `sdk_connecting` (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:80-110 state machine), so the §6.1 SIGTERM paragraph applies only to the internal DemoteSDK, not the gateway caller.

### [spec.19.review-reliability.1]

FACT: §6.1 SIGTERM-during-sdk_connecting DemoteSDK ends the pod in `terminated`, not `failed`. Edit 12's general sentence "a failed DemoteSDK fails the pod" is safe only because no open sequence can hold the slot serialization while the pod is in sdk_connecting. — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:65; spec-changes.md:321

### [spec.19.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security findings in spec round 19 — BECAUSE the r17->r19 diff is one Design sentence ("other than `startId`"), and a whole-staging pass found no regressed control: `session_started` and `startId` are runtime self-reports that gate only the runtime's own start and the CH-RUNTIMEOPS write order (withholding or forging them fails or mis-orders only that runtime's own session); the DemoteSDK deadline exception fails closed into the existing §4.7.1 `ConfigureWorkspace` row "pod transitions to `failed`"; a cleanup act performed without the slot serialization counts as failed, so the §5.2 reclaim hold is kept — ALTERNATIVES: (a) refiling "SPEC-8 Edit 1 does not refuse a connection accepted while no manifest is published"; rejected because the spec's model always has a nonce: §4.7.9 step 3 writes a placeholder manifest and the §4.7.6 `mcpNonce` row is Required and "written with each manifest", so an empty value arises only in code that skips the placeholder (CODE-7 item 1 already refuses it); (b) filing a failed `session_end` write that leaves an earlier session's context in a kept process; rejected because tenant pinning and the kept-process acknowledgment already cover that residual.
FACT: the empty-nonce fail-open exists in the code path only. In the spec, the placeholder manifest carries `mcpNonce` from boot. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:904 ("Adapter writes placeholder manifest"), :844 (`mcpNonce` Required, "written with each manifest"); spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1702 (INIT row).
FACT: for the residual state a kept runtime process carries across sessions, the authoritative bounds are tenant pinning and acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation. The microvm cross-tenant reuse path is pre-existing and acknowledged. Nothing in 0090 relaxes either bound. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:443, :458, :560
